If structureReview fires, follow §23.1 in order: recovery audit, then realized-volume audit, then a +1 set candidate under §26. The +1 set must be defined per lift first:
- SQ has no volume slot.
- In v1.4, PULL has no normal-session volume slot. The only levers are micro 1x6 or the §7.6 revert to 2x6.
- BP volume-slot sets are at about 70% load, so they would not add heavy sets.

I re-queried the Dev account's plan-2 sets and runtime state in the DB (read-only SELECTs; filter user 6f47ec53%, plan "REF5 Adaptive Strength 프로그램 2") and checked spec §7.2-7.4, §19.3, §21.1 and §23.1.

The claim's numbers are right. The brief's record is out of date: it says 13 sessions and an 8-day gap through 10/14, but there is a 14th session on 10/14 at 22:01 KST (SQ H3 85 PASS, BP focus 85 PASS, OAP R rung 2 3+2, OHP 32.5 PASS). The claim's figures use 14 sessions over 40 days:
- Sessions: 2.45/wk.
- SQ: 40 sets = 7.0/wk, of which 9 hard starts x 3 = 27 sets = 4.7/wk.
- BP: 18 focus + 12 normal-volume + 2 micro = 32 sets = 5.6/wk, focus 3.15/wk.
- PULL: 18 focus + 2 micro = 20 sets = 3.5/wk, focus 3.15/wk.
- DL, OHP and OAP pairs: about 2.1/wk each.
- "0 to 34" matches rolling 7-day windows: 34 for 9/21-9/27 and 9/05-9/11, and 0 for 10/07-10/13. Calendar weeks would give 10-24, so the claim should name the method.
- The SQ first window ran 9/05-9/29, 24 days.

Revisions needed:

(1) The "meets MED" claim is only partly true. Androulakis-Korakakis 2021 (mostly expert consensus) has three parts: 3-6 sets/wk, >80% 1RM, and RPE 7.5-9.5. REF5 meets the set count. Load is roughly met: about 18 of the 27 hard SQ sets were about 85-87%, but the Plan-B H3 sets at 82.5 kg were about 80% of the ~103-104 e1RM. PULL focus is about 80-82% of the 110 kg total, and BP 1RM is unknown. The RPE part is probably not met, because §11.1 ends sets at the first obvious slowdown, with no failure and no grinding. Robinson 2024 (load-adjusted RIR slope about 0) narrows that gap but does not close it. The claim also leans on Schoenfeld 2019 for reassurance about 3 sets/wk, but that 1-set group trained to failure at 8-12RM. Enes 2024 also used high effort. Neither study transfers cleanly to REF5's non-failure sets.

(2) Comparing the dose with the §19.3 3x/wk row is misleading at 2.45 sessions/wk. Interpolating between the 2x row (6/5/3) and the 3x row (9/7-8/4-5) gives SQ ~7.35, BP ~6.0 and PULL ~3.5-3.9. The realized dose sits on that line. So the gap comes from frequency, including the 10/07-10/13 gap: the rate is 2.84/wk over 9/05-10/06. It is not a §23.1-step-2 volume shortfall.

(3) "Every window so far ended INCREASE" holds for SQ, BP, PULL and DL. The OHP window closed MAINTAIN on 9/24 (runtime state): 4/4 PASS, but the candidate of 35 was blocked by the BP REF x 0.5 cap. That is the known §22.4 #5 issue and is not about volume, but the wording should be scoped.

The Pelland preprint knee, ACSM 2026, Ralston (1RM p=0.06) and Enes citations match the verified evidence pack. SQ at 7.0 direct sets/wk is past the ~4-set knee, not just near it. The conclusions that volume is not the near-term SQ limiter and that BP-focus and PULL heavy sets are at the low edge still hold. "Tradeoff" stays the right label.

The recommendation stays within the fixed constraints. It changes nothing now and reproduces §23.1 exactly: recovery audit, then volume audit, then +1 volume-slot set as a §26 candidate. It is a single element, does not relax FAIL 0 / HOLD<=1 / veto, and adds no accessories, RIR or AMRAP. It is under-specified in three ways:
- SQ has no volume slot.
- In v1.4, normal sessions no longer have a PULL volume slot. The OAP slot replaced it, leaving only micro 1x6 or the §7.6 revert to 2x6.
- A volume-slot set is at about 70-75%, so it does not add the heavy (>80%) sets the claim says are at the low edge.

The lever needs to be defined per lift before it goes into a §26 proposal.

## C5 · volume · tradeoff · 확신 medium · 변경 등급 none

Under the fixed constraints (no accessories, non-failure sets, a planned deficit), REF5 doses every muscle well below hypertrophy-oriented levels. Sets within about 4 RIR are roughly 4-5 per week for SQ, about 3 each for BP and PULL, and effectively 0 for DL and OHP. For a multi-year target needing about +70% squat strength at a lower bodyweight (~1.37 to 2.5x BW), this is a plausible long-run limiter. Both lenses read it as the price of C5 and C11, not a design error. They DISAGREE only on the label: A says weakness, B says tradeoff. The evidence linking hypertrophy dose to long-term 1RM in intermediates is indirect, and one 2026 trial in trained athletes found extra volume did not help.

**근거**

- ACSM 2026, MSSE 58(4):851-872: hypertrophy is enhanced by ≥10 sets per muscle per week, with a dose-response plateauing around 18-20. https://doi.org/10.1249/MSS.0000000000003897
- Schoenfeld, Ogborn & Krieger 2017, J Sports Sci 35(11):1073-1082: each additional weekly set gave about +0.37% muscle size. https://doi.org/10.1080/02640414.2016.1210197
- Robinson et al. 2024: hypertrophy rose as sets ended closer to failure; strength did not. https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40279-024-02069-2
- Murphy & Koehler 2022, Scand J Med Sci Sports 32(1):125-137: an energy deficit impaired lean-mass gains (ES -0.57) but not strength (ES -0.31, p=0.28); ~500 kcal/d prevented lean-mass gains. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34623696/ ; https://doi.org/10.1111/sms.14075
- Counterpoint: Rantila & Ahtiainen 2026, J Sci Sport Exerc: in trained athletes, 1.5x extra volume did not improve muscle size or back squat, and the deadlift favoured lower volume. https://journal.hep.com.cn/josisae/EN/10.1007/s42978-026-00387-7
- Internal record: the PULL volume 2x6 removed in v1.4 was always bodyweight +0 kg (likely ≥8 RIR), so its hypertrophy loss is small.

**추론**

Direct evidence covers short-term hypertrophy dose-response and the effect of proximity to failure. Extrapolation: that lean mass becomes the binding limiter on the way to 2.5x BW, since short-term dissociation between strength and size may not hold over years. A ~200 kcal/d deficit is well below the ~500 kcal/d threshold that abolished lean gains. Recommending accessories would violate C5, so this is reported as a cost only.

**반박 검토(evidence)**: holds-with-revision · 수정 확신 medium · 권고 유지 True

수정 주장: Under the fixed constraints and REF5's deliberate recovery-first set budget (2-3 sets per lift, V squats at about 70%, DL/OHP far from failure), REF5 delivers a low hypertrophy dose. At 2x/wk every muscle is below ACSM's ≥10 sets/wk under any counting rule. At 3-4x/wk, raw set counts for quads (9-11 squat sets plus half-value deadlift sets) and pecs approach or reach 10. The "well below" verdict then depends on counting only near-failure sets, a coaching heuristic that ACSM's threshold does not use; ACSM found failure training had no consistent effect. Estimated near-failure sets per week: SQ about 2.5-5 (RIR at H3 uncertain, about 3-6), BP focus about 3-4.5. Lats and elbow flexors get about 5-7 per side: PULL focus plus OAP pair-sets, which given FAIL/HOLD outcomes are at or near failure. That means v1.4 probably increased near-failure pulling dose while removing a low-stimulus +0 kg 2x6. DL and OHP get about 0 near-failure sets but are not zero stimulus. A ~200 kcal/d deficit proportionally attenuates, but does not abolish, lean-mass gain (Murphy & Koehler 2022). For a multi-year target of +70% squat at lower bodyweight, low hypertrophy dose is a plausible but contested long-run limiter. Short-term trials show strength is insensitive to volume above about 3-4 weekly sets in trained lifters (Schoenfeld 2019; Pelland), while Enes 2024 shows more lower-limb volume still raised squat 1RM in trained men. Whether size gains drive long-term 1RM is debated (Loenneke 2019 vs Taber 2019). Räntilä 2026 (about 40 vs 60 sets/wk) tests the top of the dose curve and does not bear on REF5's range. Classification: a tradeoff from C2/C4/C5/C11 plus a design choice, reported as a cost only.

Correct and confirmed: ACSM 2026 (≥10 sets/muscle/wk, plateau about 18-20), Schoenfeld 2017 (+0.37% per weekly set), Robinson 2024 (proximity matters for hypertrophy, not for strength), Murphy & Koehler 2022 (ES -0.57 lean mass, -0.31 strength, p=0.28, about 500 kcal/d zero point). The arithmetic is also correct: 175/103 = +70%; 103/75 = 1.37x; 175/70 = 2.5x; 0.25%/wk at 75 kg is about 206 kcal/d. The internal-record bullet matches the engine: ref5.ts line 334 computes PULL volume total as floor2.5(focus x 75/87.5). With focus at 82.5-90 kg and logged bodyweight 75, that gives +0 kg. The direction holds: REF5 is a low-hypertrophy design. But several statements overreach what the cited evidence supports.

(1) "Every muscle well below" depends on the counting rule. The claim counts only sets within about 4 RIR. ACSM's ≥10 threshold is not defined that way, and ACSM itself found training to failure "did not consistently affect outcomes". Robinson's proximity effect is a graded slope, not a cut-off at 4 RIR. Counted as raw sets (§19.3 table), quads get 9-11 squat sets at 3-4x/wk plus deadlift sets counted at half value, which reaches ACSM's ≥10. Pecs get 7-9 bench sets plus half-value OHP, which is close. "Well below for every muscle" holds firmly only at 2x/wk, or under the hard-set rule, which is a coaching heuristic.

(2) The PULL count leaves out the OAP slot. OAP outcomes were FAIL/HOLD on most exposures, so those sets are at or near failure. Hypertrophy is largely independent of load near failure (Lopez 2021; Schoenfeld 2017 low vs high load). Each lat and elbow flexor therefore gets about 2-3 more hard sets per week, giving about 5-7, not about 3. So v1.4 probably raised the near-failure pulling dose rather than lowering it. The claim's point that the lost 2x6 at +0 kg was a weak stimulus is right, but it is only half the picture.

(3) The hard-set counts rest on RIR estimates that have not been validated. At H3 85 kg (about 82% of an old e1RM), RIR is about 5-6 by Nuzzo's model and about 3 if anchored to the 92.5x3 RPE 9-10 set. SQ therefore falls anywhere from about 2.6 to 5.25 sets/wk (1.75 hard exposures x 3 sets), not a firm 4-5. "About 3" for BP and PULL is the 2x/wk figure. The observed rate of about 2.9 sessions/wk gives about 4.

(4) Räntilä & Ahtiainen 2026 is described accurately, but it is the wrong counterpoint. Its high-volume arm aimed at about 60 sets/wk vs about 40, so it tests the top of the dose curve and says nothing about REF5's low range. The relevant counterpoints are Schoenfeld 2019 MSSE (3 = 9 = 15 weekly squat/bench sets for 1RM in trained men over 8 weeks) and Pelland 2025/26 (strength returns diminish steeply). Enes 2024 MSSE, where more lower-limb volume gave more squat 1RM in trained men, cuts the other way and supports the claim's direction. It is missing from the claim.

(5) Murphy & Koehler: if the regression is roughly linear to its 500 kcal/d zero point, a 200 kcal/d deficit attenuates lean-mass gain proportionally (about 40%). It does not leave it intact, so "well below the threshold" understates the compounding cost. The included studies were mostly non-athletes. Strength ES -0.31 is non-significant from few studies, not a demonstrated null.

(6) Whether hypertrophy drives long-term 1RM is genuinely contested. Loenneke et al. 2019 and Taber et al. 2019 (both Sports Med 49(7)) argue opposite sides. I recall these papers but could not re-verify them this session because the web-search budget was exhausted. The claim's "indirect" label is appropriate, and "limiter" should stay at "plausible".

(7) Attributing this only to C5 and C11 is incomplete. The per-slot set counts (2-3 sets per lift, about 10 per session), the ~70% V squats, and the far-from-failure DL/OHP are REF5 design choices, made for C2/C4 recovery. Neither C5 nor C11 forces them, and the claim's own recommendation names a set lever. The deficit is the athlete's own cut plan (G1/recomp), not C5 or C11.

The recommendation ("none; cost only; §23.1 step 3 via §26 after audit") breaks no fixed principle and changes nothing. Calling §23.1 step 3 the "only" volume lever is slightly overstated: realized session frequency (§19.3) and the revert input (§7.6) also change weekly sets.

**반박 검토(applicability)**: holds-with-revision · 수정 확신 medium · 권고 유지 True

수정 주장: Under the fixed constraints (five lifts plus the OAP slot, no accessories, non-failure sets, and a slow cut after the first evaluation gate), REF5's hypertrophy dose is low for most muscles.

Counting only near-failure sets (a heuristic with no validated cutoff; hard-slot RIR is roughly 2-5), the weekly dose is:
- SQ: about 5-6 hard sets (6 at most under the 168 h rule).
- BP: about 3-4 focus sets.
- Lats and elbow flexors: about 3-4 PULL focus sets plus about 2 near-limit OAP sets per arm, so about 5-6. v1.4 probably increased this, because the 2x6 it replaced was bodyweight +0.
- DL and OHP: little. Their sets are far from failure, so the stimulus is reduced but not zero.

Counting raw sets the way ACSM's ≥10 figure does, quads are borderline (about 8/wk observed, 9-11 at 3-4 sessions/wk). Upper-body muscles are clearly below.

For a multi-year target of about +70% squat strength while going from about 1.37x to 2.5x bodyweight, limited lean-mass accrual is a plausible long-run limiter. This is an extrapolation: the direct evidence covers only short-term hypertrophy dose-response, and short-term strength is insensitive to proximity to failure. The planned deficit of about 200 kcal/d is below the ~500 kcal/d level at which lean-mass gains were abolished.

The low dose is a tradeoff, not a design error. Its cause is C5 and C11 together with a deliberate minimal-dose design chosen for C2 (recovery) and C4. More sets of the same lifts would not violate C5 or C11. Räntilä 2026 tested the upper limit of volume, so it is not a counterpoint at REF5's volumes.

Recommendation: report this as a cost only. The levers that need no protocol change are session frequency within C4 and attendance and scheduling (§19.3/§19.4, §23.1 step 2). A +1 set on a volume slot (§23.1 step 3) is a protocol change. It should be proposed through §26 only after a structureReview and a clean recovery audit.

I checked the claim against docs/ref5-program-spec.md (§3, §5–7, §10–15, §19–23) and the engine (packages/core/src/program-engine/ref5.ts: SQ 3x3/3x2/3x5, BP 3x3 + 2x5, DL 2x4, OHP 2x6, OAP 2x3 per arm, derived formulas at lines 332-334). The prescriptions match the spec. The core thesis survives: under the constraints, REF5's hypertrophy dose is low, the link to long-run 1RM is indirect, and this is a tradeoff. Several specifics are wrong or incomplete, though.

What holds:
- Arithmetic: 175/103 is +70%; 103/75 = 1.37x and 175/70 = 2.5x; 0.25%/wk at 75 kg is about 206 kcal/d, well below Murphy & Koehler's ~500 kcal/d threshold.
- BP and PULL focus at about 3 sets/wk matches the record. The 5 OAP exposures from 9/07 to 10/04 imply about 5 BP-focus sessions in about 4.4 weeks, so roughly 3.4 sets/wk.
- DL 2x4 at 75 kg and OHP 2x6 at 32.5 kg (about 65% of OHP REF 50) are plausibly far from failure.
- Cited evidence is accurate: ACSM 2026 (≥10 sets, plateau 18-20), Schoenfeld 2017 (+0.37% per set), Robinson 2024.
- The internal-record note is correct by formula: under v1.3, floor2.5(82.5 or 85 × 75/87.5) = 70 or 72.5 total, which is below the 75 kg bodyweight, so the added load was +0.

Problems:
(1) OAP is left out. The record shows OAP FAIL and HOLD at rungs 1-2, so these sets end at or near technical failure. They are high-tension lat and elbow-flexor sets, roughly 2 per arm per week. Counting them, near-failure pulling for the back musculature is about 5-6 sets/wk, not about 3. v1.4 probably raised near-failure lat sets, since the 2x6 it removed was far from failure. That is still below 10, but "every muscle" and "PULL ~3" understate the dose.
(2) The cutoff of "sets within about 4 RIR" is an effective-reps heuristic, not an established threshold. Robinson 2024 found a shallow, continuous RIR slope; Nuckols 2019 (in the evidence pack) disputes effective reps. Hard-slot RIR is itself uncertain: about 2-5 for H3 at 82.5% and H2 at 87%, depending on whether the athlete's 92.5x3 data point or population tables are used. Counting raw sets, as ACSM's ≥10 figure does, SQ reaches 9-11 sets/wk at 3-4 sessions/wk (§19.3) and about 8 sets/wk at the observed rate. That is borderline, not "well below". "Effectively 0" for DL and OHP overstates it: the stimulus is reduced but not zero. SQ hard sets are also about 5.25/wk at 1.75 hard exposures/wk (6 maximum), not 4-5.
(3) Attribution is off. The low per-slot set counts (2-set volume slots, DL 2x4, OHP 2x6, 10-set session, micro valves, the 168 h hard cap) are REF5 design choices made within C5 and C11. They are motivated by C2 (poor recovery), C4 and the failed nSuns attempt. They are not forced by C5 and C11. §23.1 step 3 shows more sets of the same lifts would violate neither constraint. So the cost comes from C2, C5 and C11 plus a deliberate minimal-dose choice. It is still a tradeoff, not an error.
(4) The Räntilä & Ahtiainen 2026 counterpoint is misapplied. It added 1.5x volume on top of an already high baseline (aimed at about 60 sets/wk), so it tests the upper limit and says nothing about going from about 3-6 to about 10 sets.
(5) Per spec §20, the deficit starts only after the first evaluation gate and only "if needed". Also, logged bodyweight is a constant 75 kg, so whether any deficit is under way cannot be confirmed.

Recommendation: it stays inside the fixed principles. It adds no accessories, does not relax FAIL 0 / HOLD ≤1 / the volume veto, changes one element and is gated by §23.1. So recommendationOk is true. However, calling §23.1 step 3 "the only in-protocol lever" is wrong. The spec labels step 3 a protocol change that goes through §26. Levers that need no protocol change do exist: session frequency within C4 (§19.3: going from 2 to 4 sessions/wk raises SQ from 6 to about 11 sets and BP from 5 to about 8-9), and attendance and scheduling (§23.1 step 2, §19.4). The §7.6 revert adds PULL sets, but they are far from failure and give up OAP.

## C6 · volume · tradeoff · 확신 medium · 변경 등급 protocol

The fixed caps C7 (DL ≤ SQ) and C8 (OHP ≤ 0.5 x BP) are applied through REF baselines that sit below this athlete's DL and OHP capacities. Both lifts therefore run at maintenance-or-lower intensity with near-zero hard sets:
- DL: 2x4 at 75 kg is ~64-67% of e1RM. The REF baseline is 100 at 72.5 kg, against an e1RM of ~112-117.
- OHP: 2x6 at 32.5 kg is ~56-59%. It has been frozen since 8/24 through 8 straight PASS and cannot move until BP focus reaches 90 (about December). One OHP step is 7.7% of load, against ~2.9% for the main lifts.
Separately, an auxiliary window closing just before a squat increase discarded 4 PASS exposures (DL, 8/11). The lenses agree on the facts and that this is the intended price of C7/C8. They DISAGREE on the response: A says the evidence requires no change; B offers a low-priority §26 rep-progression proposal.

**근거**

- Internal record: the 8/11 DL window ended MAINTAIN by cap with 4 PASS, and the squat increase came 2 days later. The OHP window ended MAINTAIN by cap in Plan B.
- Internal computation: the OHP cap works out to OHP ≤ floor2.5(0.398 x BP focus), against a historical OHP:BP e1RM ratio of ~0.58. The next OHP step needs BP focus 90.
- Androulakis-Korakakis, Fisher & Steele 2020, Sports Med 50(4):751-765: minimum dose for trained men was 1 set of 6-12 reps at ~70-85%, to failure, 2-3 times a week; deadlift evidence is lacking. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31797219/
- Spiering et al. 2021, JSCR 35(5):1449-1458: strength was maintained at low volume when relative intensity was maintained (younger general adults). https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33629972/
- Plotkin et al. 2022, PeerJ 10:e14142: adding reps at a fixed load was a viable overload route in trained adults (8-12RM sets to failure, lower body). https://peerj.com/articles/14142/
- Rantila & Ahtiainen 2026: the deadlift favoured lower volume. https://journal.hep.com.cn/josisae/EN/10.1007/s42978-026-00387-7
- Zourdos (Stronger By Science, coaching): on a stall, repeat the load or add a rep or set. https://www.strongerbyscience.com/weekly-load-progression/

**추론**

The intensities and the window discard are record and engine facts (high confidence). That this dose maintains rather than builds DL and OHP is an extrapolation: the minimum-dose studies used sets to failure, and Spiering's population is not athletes. The athlete's 1RM OHP:BP ratio (~0.58) already exceeds C8, so the cap is meant to bind. The low priority of DL and OHP is the user's choice, so this is a cost, not a defect. Plotkin's rep-progression evidence is indirect for non-failure OHP sets, which is why A sees little expected benefit and B ranks the proposal low.

**반박 검토(evidence)**: holds-with-revision · 수정 확신 medium · 권고 유지 True

수정 주장: C7 (DL ≤ SQ) and C8 (OHP ≤ 0.5 x BP) apply through REF baselines that sit below this athlete's estimated DL and OHP capacities. This is a cost the user chose, not a defect.

DL:
- 2x4 at 75 kg is roughly 64-67% of an e1RM estimated at ~112-117. That anchor's provenance is unverified, so treat the range as ±several percent.
- These sets are far from failure: roughly ≥10 RIR, so near-zero hard sets.
- DL is not frozen. Its cap tracks squat: DL max = floor2.5(0.914 x SQ H3), which is 77.5 kg at the current SQ H3 of 85. It rises whenever an all-PASS window closes under the current cap.
- The engine-confirmed cost is that a window closing just before a squat increase is judged against the old cap and then emptied. The 8/11 DL window with 4 PASS is an example (record not independently re-verified).

OHP:
- 2x6 at 32.5 kg is roughly 56-59% of e1RM.
- It is frozen by the cap. OHP max = floor2.5(0.398 x BP focus), so the 35 kg candidate needs BP focus 90, two more BP increases away (late Nov to Dec if all windows pass).
- The 2.5 kg step is 7.7% of the working load but only ~4.4% of e1RM. The binding limit is the cap, not the step size.

Whether this dose maintains or slowly builds DL/OHP cannot be read off the cited evidence:
- The minimum-dose data (Androulakis-Korakakis 2020) used sets to failure.
- Spiering 2021 (general adults) makes maintained relative intensity the key variable, which argues against calling ~56-67% non-failure sets 'maintenance'.
- Transfer from the heavy SQ and BP work could partly offset the low direct dose; it is unquantified.
- Räntilä & Ahtiainen 2026 compares ~40 vs ~60 weekly sets and does not inform a 2-set/wk DL dose.

No change is required by the evidence. If OHP progress inside C8's REF inequality is wanted, the single §26-style candidate is OHP rep progression at fixed load (2x6 to 2x8 under the existing all-PASS rule). Its support is indirect: Plotkin 2022 tested lower-body sets taken to failure, measured 1RM on a Smith machine, and favoured load progression by ~2 kg (CI crossing zero). Expect little or no effect from sets that stay ~14 RIR. Judgment criteria are unchanged.

WHAT I VERIFIED

Engine and spec (read-only):
- packages/core/src/program-engine/ref5.ts lines 348-357 (deriveRef5AuxiliaryCaps) and 2376-2445; docs/ref5-program-spec.md §6.3, §6.4, §13.3, §16.
- OHP cap: OHP max = floor2.5(BP REF x 0.5 x 32.5/50) = floor2.5(0.3979 x BP focus). The 0.398 figure is correct. The REF inequality is grid-free:
  - BP 85 or 87.5 -> REF/2 = 52.0 or 53.6, below the 35 kg candidate's REF of 53.85, so it is rejected.
  - BP 90 -> 55.1, so it passes.
  - So "next OHP step needs BP focus 90" is correct.
- Step sizes: 2.5/32.5 = 7.7% and 2.5/85 = 2.9%. Both correct.
- Window discard: when the 4-exposure window completes, the engine empties it whatever the outcome, and the cap is checked against the caps in force at that completion (§16 steps 5-7). An all-PASS window that hits the cap therefore discards those PASSes. If a squat increase lands 2 days later, the DL window must refill from zero. The "discarded 4 PASS" mechanism is confirmed.
- Intensity arithmetic is internally correct: 75/117-112 = 64-67%; 32.5/58-55 = 56-59%.

Not verified:
- I could not check the record facts myself. The Supabase read was denied by the permission classifier, so I did not query the DB. Unchecked: the 8/11 date, OHP frozen since 8/24 with 8 straight PASS, Plan B MAINTAIN, and where the e1RM anchors come from (DL ~112-117, OHP:BP ~0.58).
- The shared web-search budget was exhausted, so I could not search for counter-evidence. I fetched Räntilä and Plotkin directly.

CITATIONS: accuracy and how far each supports the claim
- Androulakis-Korakakis 2020: accurate (1 set of 6-12 reps at ~70-85%, to failure, 2-3x/wk; deadlift data lacking). It establishes a minimum dose that was observed to work. It does not show that lower doses fail. Using it to say REF5's DL/OHP dose "maintains rather than builds" is an extrapolation, and the claim says so.
- Spiering 2021: accurate (general younger adults, data insufficient for athletes). Its main point is that relative intensity must be kept for strength to be maintained. No source defines ~56-67% non-failure sets as "maintenance intensity". If anything, Spiering would predict slow loss of lift-specific 1RM if earlier DL/OHP training was heavier. "Maintenance-or-lower" is acceptable only because "or lower" is included.
- Plotkin 2022 (fetched): accurate. Two qualifications strengthen the claim's own caveat:
  - the REPS group added reps to sets taken to momentary failure;
  - 1RM was tested on a Smith machine and favoured LOAD by 2.0 kg (90% CI crosses zero).
  So there is no direct support for rep progression in sets that stay ~14 RIR (2x6 to 2x8 at ~57% e1RM).
- Räntilä & Ahtiainen 2026 (fetched): the finding is accurate (27 athletes; extra volume = 1.5x sets, aiming for ~60 sets/wk; only DL differed, favouring less volume, P=0.043). It does not bear on this claim. It compares very high whole-program volumes, an order of magnitude above 2 DL sets/wk, and says nothing about whether 2x4 at ~65% is enough. Drop it, or label it irrelevant to dose adequacy.
- Zourdos/SBS: coaching opinion, cited accurately.

OVERSTATEMENTS AND OMISSIONS
1. DL is coupled to SQ, not frozen like OHP. DL cap = floor2.5(0.914 x SQ H3). At SQ H3 85 the cap is 77.5, so DL at 75 can step up on its next all-PASS window. Only OHP is frozen. Putting DL and OHP under one "frozen" framing overstates the DL cost. The DL cost is (a) a low relative intensity by construction (~72.5% of its REF vs ~79% for SQ H3) and (b) occasional discarded windows.
2. The outcome inference ("maintenance-or-lower") leaves out transfer from the heavy SQ and BP work. The specificity meta-analyses in the pack (Spitz 2023: non-trained-test d 0.8 vs 1.84 trained) imply non-zero but smaller transfer. DL/OHP capacity could therefore drift up even with this low direct dose. No source quantifies SQ->DL or BP->OHP transfer, so the outcome is uncertain in both directions.
3. The step of 7.7% of load is large only relative to a load already at ~57% of e1RM. Measured against e1RM it is ~4.4%, versus ~2.4% for SQ. A step would move OHP from ~57% to ~61%, which is unlikely to cause HOLD/FAIL. The binding factor is the cap, not the step. The claim mostly says this, but the 7.7% vs 2.9% comparison suggests a difficulty problem that the evidence does not show.
4. The percent-of-e1RM figures depend on unverified, possibly old e1RM anchors (likely Epley from logs) and carry several percentage points of uncertainty. "Near-zero hard sets" still holds under any reasonable anchor, because Nuzzo 2024 implies ≥15 reps possible at ≤67%.

THE RECOMMENDATION
- It stays inside the user's principles: no change required. Its optional element is a single-element §26-style candidate (OHP 2x6 to 2x8 at fixed load under the existing all-PASS rule). It does not relax FAIL 0 / HOLD≤1 / veto, uses no AMRAP and adds no accessories.
- It meets the REF letter of C8, because REF is load-only.
- Whether growing OHP capacity while the cap holds fits the intent behind C8 is the user's call.
- The "small expected effect" caveat is correct and, given that Plotkin's sets went to failure, perhaps generous.

수정 주장: C7 (DL ≤ SQ) and C8 (OHP ≤ 0.5 × BP) are enforced through fixed REF coefficients (§6.3–6.4), so DL working load is pinned at ≤ floor2.5(0.914 × SQ H3) and OHP at ≤ floor2.5(0.398 × BP focus).

- OHP (32.5 kg) is frozen by the cap. At BP 85 and 87.5 the 35 kg candidate is rejected. OHP and BP-focus windows fill in the same sessions, so the earliest OHP step is the session in which BP focus reaches 90. As of 10/14 that is 7 more BP-focus exposures, roughly late November to December if every BP window ends INCREASE. Long term, OHP can step at most once per ~2.5 BP increases. Each OHP step is 7.7% of load, against ~2.9% for the main lifts.
- DL (75 kg) is not cap-bound at this moment (its cap is 77.5 kg at SQ H3 85). It is tied to the squat, though, and the engine clears an auxiliary window even when it ends MAINTAIN by cap, with no carry-over. DL windows that close before the next SQ step therefore discard their PASS exposures. This happened on 8/11 and probably again around 9/23 (inferred from the handoff state).
- Relative intensity is uncertain. On the claim's undocumented e1RMs (DL ~112–117, OHP ~55–58) the working loads are ~64–67% and ~56–59%. On the user's own documented inputs (DL 100, OHP 50) they are ~75% and ~65%. Either way, the sets end far from failure under §11.1, as every REF5 slot does.
- Whether this dose maintains or builds DL/OHP strength is extrapolation. The gap also narrows mechanically, because auxiliary loads rise with SQ and BP.

This is the intended cost of C7/C8, not a defect. No change is required. If the user ever wants OHP to progress while the cap holds, the single §26-style candidate is fixed-load rep progression (2×6 → 2×8) under the unchanged all-PASS rule, with a reset to 2×6 when a load step becomes allowed. It respects C8's wording (a weight cap) but works against C8's auxiliary intent, and its expected benefit is small and based on indirect evidence.

I checked the claim against the spec (docs/ref5-program-spec.md §5.1, §6.3–6.4, §13.3, §16, §22.4 #5), the engine (packages/core/src/program-engine/ref5.ts: deriveRef5AuxiliaryCaps, ref5AuxiliaryCandidateIsWithinCap, the auxiliary-window block at ~L2377–2440), the 2026-10-14 handoffs and the constraints sheet in /mnt/attach/project-files. My direct DB read was denied, so record dates and counts are checked only for consistency with the handoff state.

CONFIRMED (rules and arithmetic):
(1) The OHP cap reduces to OHP ≤ floor2.5(0.3979 × BP focus). That gives 32.5 at BP 85 and at BP 87.5, and 35 only at BP 90.
(2) One OHP step is 7.7% of the load (2.5/32.5); one SQ step is 2.9% (2.5/85).
(3) DL REF is 100 at 72.5 kg (§6.3).
(4) The engine clears the DL/OHP window after it is judged, even when the result is MAINTAIN because of the cap, and it carries no credit forward. So a DL window that closes before an SQ step loses its 4 PASS. The 8/11 → SQ-increase-two-days-later sequence fits the old plan's ~25-day first SQ window.
(5) In Plan B, OHP and BP-focus windows fill in the same sessions. The 9/24 OHP window was judged after BP rose (§16 steps 5–7) and still ended MAINTAIN by cap.

REVISIONS NEEDED:
(a) The e1RM denominators (DL ~112–117, OHP ~55–58, OHP:BP ~0.58) are not in the spec, the constraints sheet or either handoff, and I could not check them in the DB. The only documented values are the user's own appendix inputs, "입력 1RM: … DL 100 · OHP 50". On those, DL 75 kg is ~75% and OHP 32.5 kg is ~65%. The reasoning calls the intensities "record and engine facts (high confidence)". Only the working loads are facts; the percentages depend on an unverified source. OHP 50 ≈ 0.5 × 101 does suggest the input was cap-shaped rather than measured, which fits the claim, but it is not proof.
(b) DL is not cap-bound right now. At SQ H3 85 the DL cap is floor2.5(107.15 × 0.725) = 77.5, and DL is 75. The structural point is that DL is pinned at roughly 0.914 × SQ H3. Because DL and SQ windows run out of sync, discards recur. The handoff state (DL still 75 after a closed Plan-B DL window and the 9/29 SQ step) suggests a second discard around 9/23, but that is inferred, not verified. The cap also does not loosen at SQ H3 87.5 (DL cap stays 77.5).
(c) "Maintenance-or-lower" is an extrapolation and should not be stated as a fixed state. Auxiliary loads rise with the main lifts (DL ~0.91 × the SQ step, OHP ~0.40 × the BP step), so the relative-intensity gap narrows unless DL/OHP capacity grows too. OHP can move at most once per ~2.5 BP increases. Its earliest step is the session in which BP reaches 90, which is 7 more BP-focus exposures after the 10/04 one (late November to December only if every BP window ends INCREASE).
(d) "Near-zero hard sets" holds for every REF5 slot under the stop-at-slowdown rule (§11.1). It is not special to C7/C8; what the caps add is the lower relative load.
(e) "8 straight PASS since 8/24" is plausible: 5 OHP exposures on Plan-B OAP days plus about 3 from the old plan. I could not verify it. Using Räntilä 2026 (extra volume on top of a very high baseline) as relevant to a 2-set DL dose is a stretch.

RECOMMENDATION: within the fixed principles.
- "No change required" fits the evidence.
- The optional OHP rep progression (2×6 → 2×8 at a fixed load while the cap holds) adds no accessory, AMRAP or RIR, and does not relax FAIL 0 / HOLD ≤1 / veto (a higher rep target is stricter).
- It respects the constraints sheet's wording of C8 ("무게 상한 = 벤치의 50%", a weight cap).
- It is one protocol element, presented as a conditional §26-style candidate.
- Caveats it should carry: OHP REF is defined on load only, so rep gains are invisible to the cap. That works against C8's "보조" (auxiliary) intent, since actual OHP:BP may already exceed 0.5. It also needs sub-rules for rep steps and for resetting to 2×6 when a load step is allowed. Its evidence base (Plotkin 2022: lower body, sets to failure, 8–12RM; plus coaching sources) is indirect, as the claim itself says.

## C7 · progression/windows · tradeoff · 확신 medium · 변경 등급 doc-or-display

The 6- and 4-exposure windows with +2.5 kg steps are a conservative, noise-tolerant progression filter. Their cadence ceiling (~43 kg/yr of H3 in theory, ~34 kg/yr at the observed 3.8 weeks per window) is about 1.3-2.1x the best yearly squat gains of competitive powerlifters (20-25 kg/yr). So cadence is not the long-run limiter; window outcome quality is. Squat, the top priority, has the longest window under the same HOLD ≤1 / FAIL 0 gate. At equal per-exposure noise its windows therefore pass less often: 0.46 vs 0.65 for BP/PULL at P(HOLD)=0.2 and P(FAIL)=0.05. Squat will show MAINTAIN windows first and most often. The short-term cost is lag during the current re-climb: windows took 17-25 days. The lenses DISAGREE on the label: A says strength, B says tradeoff.

**근거**

- Grgic et al. 2020, Sports Med Open 6:31: 1RM test-retest CV in trained lifters is 3.3%, about one 2.5 kg step at 85 kg, so multi-exposure windows damp single-session noise. https://link.springer.com/article/10.1186/s40798-020-00260-z
- Latella et al. 2022, MSSE 54(5):841-850 (1,896 competitors): maximum yearly gains of squat 20.2-25.4 kg, deadlift 18.1-21.1 kg, bench 10.5-12.8 kg. https://doi.org/10.1249/MSS.0000000000002858
- Latella et al. 2020, JSCR: the strongest male quartile gained about half as fast as the weakest (0.102 vs 0.211 kg/day of total). https://doi.org/10.1519/JSC.0000000000003657
- Latella et al. 2024, Sports Med 54(3):753-774: about 7.5-12.5% gain in the first competitive year and up to about 20% after 10 years. https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-023-01962-6
- Nuckols 2022 (coaching analysis): each equally spaced milestone takes about twice as long as the last. https://www.strongerbyscience.com/?p=57447
- Graham & Cleather 2021: when load lags capacity, gains are smaller (+10.8% vs +7.1%). https://journals.lww.com/nsca-jscr/fulltext/2021/09000/autoregulation_by__repetitions_in_reserve__leads.14.aspx . Hickmott et al. 2022, Sports Med Open 8:9: autoregulated vs fixed load for 1RM MD +2.07 kg (CI -0.32 to 4.46), not significant. https://link.springer.com/article/10.1186/s40798-021-00404-9
- Internal record: window durations SQ 25 and 24 days, BP 20 and 17, PULL 23 and 18. REF rises 3.15 per 2.5 kg H3 increment.
- Internal computation: binomial window-success, SQ 0.75^6 + 6(0.2)(0.75^5) = 0.46; BP/PULL 0.75^4 + 4(0.2)(0.75^3) = 0.65.

**추론**

Direct evidence: reliability data support matching the increment to noise. The rate benchmarks are observational data from selected competitive samples, a different population, so the ceiling-to-biology ratio is approximate. The window-length and step-size design itself has no trial and rests on arithmetic and coaching reasoning. Lag costs in autoregulation meta-analyses are small and uncertain. Implication: at plausible true rates of 3-10%/yr, many MAINTAIN windows and eventual stagnation decreases are the expected steady state. B's original recommendation said MAINTAIN windows at that rate should be read as 'predicted, not a signal to restructure'. That would conflict with the user's §21.1 pre-registration that low acquisition is first read as insufficient stimulus or recovery, so it is narrowed below.

**반박 검토(evidence)**: holds-with-revision · 수정 확신 medium · 권고 유지 True

수정 주장: The 6-exposure and 4-exposure windows with +2.5 kg steps are a conservative filter. Requiring several clean exposures stops a single good day from raising the load. Grgic 2020 puts trained 1RM day-to-day CV at about 3.3%, about one REF step, though using that figure for judgment noise is an extrapolation.

The filter is not symmetric about noise. Because the gate is a conjunction (FAIL 0, HOLD <=1, no V or volume FAIL), longer windows produce noise-driven MAINTAINs more often. At a stationary P(HOLD)=0.2 and P(FAIL)=0.05, illustrative pass rates are 0.46 for SQ and 0.65 for BP/PULL. That model ignores the vetoes, which differ by lift: SQ V, all BP volume, and micro-only PULL volume.

The cadence ceiling in 1RM-equivalent terms is about 43-55 kg/yr (H3 x 1.26). At the observed 24-25-day SQ windows it is about 37 kg/yr H3 (about 47 REF). That is roughly 1.7-2.7x the group-mean first-to-best annualized squat gains of competitive powerlifters (20.2-25.4 kg/yr, Latella 2022). Those are averages, not individual maxima, and individual variance is large. So cadence is very unlikely to limit long-run progress, though it can lag briefly if loads currently sit below capacity. Whether reserve remains is unverified: SQ REF is about 107, already above the stated ~103-104 1RM.

Because outcomes depend on how close the load is to capacity, the durable asymmetry is this: the longer SQ window should settle SQ at a somewhat more conservative margin below capacity than BP/PULL. It does not follow that SQ will have the most MAINTAIN windows. In steady state, BP/PULL close more windows per year and gain fewer kg, so they may show as many or more.

At realistic rates, MAINTAIN windows and periodic stagnation decreases are the expected steady state for every main lift. Realistic rates are front-loaded: about 7.5-12.5% in a first competitive year, about 3%/yr median by later meets, and about 20% cumulative over 10 years (Latella 2024; Nuckols 2018).

No protocol change. If a benchmark is added to the 23.1 audit, label it as near-term about 3-10%/yr, decaying toward about 2-3%/yr. Record it only as context; it must not be used to override the 21.1 first reading.

The core claim holds: the windows are conservative, cadence is not the long-run limiter, and no protocol change is warranted. But several statements are stated more strongly than the evidence or arithmetic allows.

1) "Best yearly squat gains" mislabels Latella 2022.
- The abstract says the maximum gain was computed by comparing each lifter's first and maximum competition scores, then annualized. 20.2-25.4 kg/yr is a group-mean first-to-best rate, not an upper bound on any individual.
- Individual variance is very large. Latella 2020 reports males at 0.15 +/- 0.44 kg/day of total.
- So "1.3-2.1x the best gains" does not show that no lifter could out-gain the cadence. It shows the cadence comfortably exceeds typical competitive rates.

2) The ratio compares mismatched units.
- The ceiling is in H3 kg, but Latella's figures are 1RM gains. One +2.5 kg H3 step raises REF by 3.15 kg (x1.26).
- In 1RM-equivalent terms the ceiling is about 43-55 kg/yr, which is about 1.7-2.7x the Latella means. The claim understates its own conclusion.
- "Observed 3.8 weeks/window" does not match the internal record. SQ windows of 24-25 days are about 3.5 weeks, which gives about 37 kg/yr H3, not 34.

3) "Noise-tolerant" is only half true.
- Grgic 2020 (trained 1RM CV 3.3%, about one REF step) supports not increasing on one good day. But that is 1RM test-retest noise; applying it to PASS/HOLD judgments is extrapolation.
- Because the gate is a conjunction (FAIL 0 AND HOLD <=1 AND no V/volume FAIL), a longer window makes a noise-induced MAINTAIN more likely. The claim's own 0.46 vs 0.65 arithmetic is correct and shows exactly this.
- The accurate label is: damps false increases, but is sensitive to noise-driven MAINTAINs, more so as the window gets longer.

4) "Same gate" is not identical across lifts (spec 13.1-13.2).
- SQ has its own V-FAIL veto.
- BP volume FAIL counts every normal and micro BP volume exposure.
- PULL's veto input is micro-only in v1.4.

5) "Squat will show MAINTAIN windows first and most often" holds only under a stationary, equal-noise binomial model.
- In REF5's adaptive loop, P(HOLD) depends on how close the load is to capacity. In steady state, MAINTAIN windows per year are roughly windows per year minus increments per year.
- BP/PULL close more windows per year (record: 17-23 days vs 24-25), and bench gains fewer kg per year (Latella 2022: 10.5-12.8 vs 20.2-25.4). So BP/PULL can show as many or more MAINTAINs per year.
- The defensible structural point is different: at the same gate, the longer SQ window settles SQ load at a more conservative margin below capacity.
- "First" is only a modest expectation. It competes with the other dimensions' PULL-first hypothesis.

6) The recommendation's "~3-10%/yr long-run" benchmark contradicts sources the claim itself cites.
- Latella 2024 (abstract verified): about 7.5-12.5% in the first competitive year, and up to about 20% cumulative after 10 years, which averages about 2%/yr.
- Nuckols 2018: median rate fell to 0.26%/month (about 3%/yr) by meets 4-5.
- 3-10%/yr is a near-term band for an intermediate, not a long-run rate.

7) "Lag during the current re-climb" assumes reserve that is not established.
- SQ REF is already about 107 (85 x 104/82.5), above the stated ~103-104 1RM.
- The evidence for a lag cost is one RCT (Graham & Cleather) plus a non-significant meta-analysis (Hickmott, MD 2.07 kg, CI -0.32 to 4.46). Zero HOLD/FAIL cannot tell lag apart from an insensitive detector.

Recommendation: OK. It is documentation-only, changes no gate, respects 21.1 and 23.1, and stays within one element. The benchmark wording must be corrected as in revisedClaim.

**반박 검토(applicability)**: holds-with-revision · 수정 확신 medium · 권고 유지 True

수정 주장: REF5's 6- (SQ hard) and 4-exposure (BP/PULL focus) windows with +2.5 kg steps act as a conservative filter. They damp single-session noise against false increases, at the cost of more false MAINTAINs. Their cadence ceiling is about 43.5 kg/yr of H3 in theory, about 55 kg/yr in REF/1RM-equivalent units. The observed cadence is about 28-31 kg/yr H3 (about 35-39 REF-equivalent): 4.2-4.7 weeks per SQ window, about 1.3-1.45 hard SQ per week, increase-to-increase cycles of 29 and 34 days. Compared like-for-like, that is roughly 1.4-2.7x the best yearly squat 1RM gains of competitive powerlifters (20-25 kg/yr). Cadence is therefore unlikely to be the long-run limiter; window outcome quality is.

Under independent exposures with equal per-exposure noise (P(HOLD)=0.2, P(FAIL)=0.05), SQ windows pass about 0.46 versus 0.65 for BP/PULL. The volume veto lowers all three, most for SQ (about 5 V exposures per window) and least for PULL (micro volume only). The ordering is therefore SQ < BP < PULL, and SQ is expected to log the most MAINTAIN windows. That prediction depends on assumptions that may not hold: noise differs by lift (H2 is the heaviest relative slot; PULL is exposed to bodyweight, climbing and OAP), and HOLDs may cluster. Whether current loads lag capacity cannot be determined from the all-PASS record, since H3 85 already implies REF 107, above the stated ~103-104 1RM.

Rules check: the claim matches the spec and the engine. Spec §13.1/§13.2 and ref5.ts:2302-2308 define the windows as 6 valid normal H3/H2 for SQ and 4 normal focus exposures for BP/PULL. Increase is +2.5 kg only if FAIL=0, HOLD<=1 and the volume-FAIL veto list is empty. Hard SQ requires >=48 h and <2 hard starts in the open 168 h window (ref5.ts:1198-1203). So the theoretical ceiling is 3 wk/window, 17.4 windows/yr and 43.5 kg/yr of H3. The binomial arithmetic is correct: SQ 0.463 vs BP/PULL 0.654.

Records check: I verified read-only against plan_progress_event in prod Supabase. Window spans match the claim: SQ 25 and 24 d, BP 20 and 17 d, PULL 23 and 18 d. Every window ended INCREASE, and there is still no HOLD/FAIL. A normal session on 10/14 13:01 completed SQ H3 PASS, so the current SQ window is 3/6.

Problems found:
(1) The cadence is overstated. The 3.8 wk/window figure only holds for 09-05..10-06, which leaves out the 8-day gap.
- v1.4 overall: 9 hard SQ in 39 d, about 1.44/wk, so 4.2 wk/window.
- Old plan: 9 in 44 d, about 1.27/wk, so 4.7 wk/window.
- Actual increase-to-increase cycles were 29 and 34 d.
- The relay's '1.75 hard/wk' is also too high.
Observed H3 cadence is about 28-31 kg/yr, which is roughly 1.1-1.5x the 20.2-25.4 kg/yr competitor maximum (Latella 2022) in raw H3 kg. The comparison should be like-for-like: Latella reports 1RM gains, and REF rises 3.15 per 2.5 kg H3 step. In those units the ceiling is about 35-39 kg/yr observed and 55 kg/yr theoretical, i.e. about 1.4-2.7x. The conclusion 'cadence not the long-run limiter' survives, but only after this conversion.

(2) The binomial leaves out the volume veto, which REF5 applies to all three lifts with different exposure counts.
- SQ: V normal + micro, 5 in the v1.4 SQ window.
- BP: volume normal + micro, 4.
- PULL: micro only, 1 (§13.2, §22.4 #10).
Example at P(volume FAIL)=0.02: SQ 0.42, BP 0.60, PULL 0.64. BP and PULL are therefore not equal; PULL's gate is the most lenient. The SQ-lowest ranking is strengthened.

(3) 'Squat will show MAINTAIN first and most often' only follows under the stated equal-noise, independent-exposure assumption. It should not be stated unconditionally.
- Per-exposure noise is unequal. H2 sits at about 84% of control REF versus 79% for H3 and BP focus, which favors the claim. PULL total load varies with bodyweight, climbing and ~24 h OAP adjacency, and the evidence pack hypothesizes PULL may falter first.
- HOLDs plausibly cluster (sleep, gaps).
- In calendar time the window cycles are similar across lifts (SQ 29-34 d, BP 27-31 d, PULL 24-34 d), because SQ gets more hard exposures per week than BP/PULL get focus exposures. 'Longest window' is true in exposure count, not much in time.

(4) 'Lag during the current re-climb' is a hypothesis. H3 85 gives a control REF of 107.2, already above the stated ~103-104 1RM. The 0 HOLD/FAIL record cannot separate 'loads lag capacity' from a conservative but adequate load or an insensitive slowdown call.

(5) The benchmark '~3-10%/yr' fits early competitive years: Nuckols 2018 mean ~0.58%/mo; Latella 2024 7.5-12.5% in year 1. Multi-year averages are lower: Latella 2024 up to ~20% over 10 yr; Nuckols median 0.26%/mo by meets 4-5. It should be labelled as an early-years competitor benchmark.

Recommendation check: no protocol change, a single doc/context note, the gate is not relaxed, §21.1 and the §23.1 order are kept, and any SQ window change is deferred to a single §26 candidate. Per §26.1/§26.2, a doc note is not a protocol change, so it does not violate the fixed constraints and is not bundled. Nit: the note fits better in §21/§22.4 than inside the ordered §23.1 procedure, so it is not read as an extra step.

All logged kg-lift exposures PASS and every SQ/BP/PULL window ended INCREASE (internal data, not independently verified here). Current SQ/BP/PULL standards remain at or below the athlete's June 2026 working loads (SQ H3 85 / H2 90 vs 92.5x3x3, which was probably near-maximal; BP 85 vs 90; PULL added +15 vs +19). So these outcomes were expected whatever the program's effectiveness. The squat may hit its first discriminating zone within about 1 increment (H3 87.5 / H2 92.5), not 2-3. DL and OHP are governed by caps (DL max 77.5 at H3 85; OHP frozen at 32.5 by the BP cap), so their windows say nothing about physiological rate, and the DL 100 / OHP 50 'June' figures, which equal the initial control REFs, should be verified or dropped. Recommendation (documentation only, criteria unchanged): in the §21.1 review, treat SQ/BP/PULL INCREASE windows as catch-up until the standard reaches the June load (specify 'reaches' or 'exceeds' explicitly). Treat any HOLD, FAIL or MAINTAIN below that load as informative. Exclude DL/OHP from the rule.

The core inference holds, but it rests on logic and the training record, not on research. If current SQ/BP/PULL standards sit below loads the athlete already handled in June 2026, an unbroken run of PASS/INCREASE is expected whether or not REF5 is working. So the record cannot yet measure adaptation rate. Several specifics are overstated or wrong.

1) Halonen 2024. I checked the PubMed abstract. It studied 55 UNTRAINED adults (42 completed), with 10 weeks of training, 10 weeks of detraining, then 10 weeks of retraining. Leg-press 1RM and muscle size gains in the first 5 retraining weeks beat a control group's first 5 weeks. The claim describes this result accurately, but it transfers weakly. It needs real detraining, and nothing in the claim shows any detraining between the June Tactical Barbell block and the 7/19 start. The defensible mechanism is catching up to capacity already shown, which means sets below that capacity. That inference needs no citation, and 're-acquisition' presumes a loss that was never shown.

2) DL 75 vs 100 and OHP 32.5 vs 50. The cited internal evidence lists June loads only for SQ/BP/PULL. The DL/OHP 'June 3x3' figures equal the initial control REFs exactly (REF5_INITIAL_CONTROL_REFS_KG: DL 100, OHP 50 in ref5.ts). So they may be REFs, not loads; I could not verify this because DB access was denied. Either way they are irrelevant, because the caps bind. Per deriveRef5AuxiliaryCaps, at SQ H3 85 the DL max is 77.5, and DL 100 needs SQ REF >= 137.9, i.e. H3 ~110. At BP 85 the OHP max is 32.5, so OHP is frozen now, and OHP 50 needs BP ~126. DL/OHP windows depend on the caps and say nothing about physiology either way. The proposed pre-registration should exclude them.

3) 'First informative zone 2-3 increments away' is likely too far. It compares H3 only and leaves out H2: current H2 is 90x2, only 2.5 kg under the June 92.5x3x3. The athlete's history (92.5x3 @ RPE 9-10; TM had drifted up to real capacity) suggests the June squat sets were near-maximal grinds, so they mark a capacity ceiling, not a comfortable PASS level. REF5's PASS rule (no obvious slowdown) is stricter, so HOLD/FAIL can plausibly appear about 1 increment from now (H3 87.5 / H2 92.5). The 10/04 'strength wasn't coming' memo fits this.

4) The claim contradicts itself. It puts the informative zone at H3 90-92.5, but the recommendation counts windows only once the standard EXCEEDS 92.5. That means H3 95: 4 increments, about 14 weeks plus a window.

5) PULL's 4 kg gap (90 vs 94 total) is within the error from the logged bodyweight, which is likely a default 75. Comparing added load (+15 vs +19) is safer.

6) Make the asymmetry explicit: INCREASE windows below the June loads carry no information about rate, but a HOLD, FAIL or MAINTAIN below them is informative.

The 106/106 PASS count, the 6/6 INCREASE windows and the DL/OHP cap-forced MAINTAINs are internal data. I could not verify them (DB query denied), but they match the brief. Web search budget was exhausted; only the Halonen abstract was fetched.

## C9 · measurement · weakness · 확신 medium · 변경 등급 operational

The binary 'obvious slowdown' judgment is low-resolution at current loads:
- 2-3-rep sets at ~82-88% end before a visible slowdown would normally appear.
- Perceived velocity loss carries about 1 rep of error, worse above 60% 1RM, and is barely perceived in the deadlift.
- The §11 tie-break ('if ambiguous, record no slowdown') sends borderline sets to PASS.
The lenses DISAGREE on the direction of bias. B judges it probably biased toward PASS, because the tie-break dominates. A holds the net direction uncertain, because lifters also underpredict reps to failure, which pushes toward conservative calls. Both agree that at RIR ~3-5 PASS may simply be correct. Both also agree that HOLD ≤1 absorbs isolated noise, so clustered HOLDs carry the signal.

**근거**

- Dello Iacono et al. 2023, IJSPP 18(5):488-494: mean absolute error of perceived 20% and 40% velocity loss on bench was about 1 rep, and worse above 60% 1RM. https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2022-0298
- Sindiani et al. 2020, Physiol Behav 224:113025: perception error grew across reps; squat velocity change was 4.2x more likely to be underestimated (at 60-70% loads). https://doi.org/10.1016/j.physbeh.2020.113025
- Shaw et al. 2023, Int J Strength Cond: in the deadlift, only a 10.49% probability of perceiving ≥20% velocity loss better than chance (n=17). https://journal.iusca.org/index.php/Journal/article/view/161
- Halperin et al. 2022, Sports Med 52:377-390: reps to failure were underpredicted by ~0.95 reps (I² 97.9%), the direction opposite to leniency. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34542869/
- Jukic et al. 2023, Eur J Appl Physiol 123:1343-1357: in the free-weight back squat, the velocity-loss vs %reps relationship did not reproduce between sessions. https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00421-023-05155-x
- Spec §11 tie-break; §22.4.7-8 already list the information lost by a binary judgment.

**추론**

Direct evidence comes from perception-accuracy studies, mostly on bench, deadlift, Smith machines or moderate loads. Mapping them onto free-weight high-bar triples at 82-88% is extrapolation. The slowdown judgment itself has not been validated in any study. The disagreement on bias direction is real and cannot be resolved from current data. Either way the remedy is more resolution, not a change to the criteria. Medium confidence.

**반박 검토(evidence)**: holds-with-revision · 수정 확신 medium · 권고 유지 True

수정 주장: The binary 'obvious slowdown' judgment carries little information at current loads.

The main reason is a floor effect, not perception noise. At H3 (~82%, roughly 5 RIR on population tables) and on V sets, a 3-rep set probably ends before a large velocity drop occurs. A NORMAL stop with 0 reps short is PASS, so with the 'obvious' threshold and the §11 tie-break ('if ambiguous, record no slowdown'), these sets default to PASS. This inference is uncited and may not hold for H2. On the athlete's own 92.5x3 @ RPE 9-10 anchor, H2 at 90 kg (~87%) sits near 2 RIR, where a slowdown should be visible by rep 2.

Perception evidence is indirect: Dello Iacono 2023 used bench across 40-90% loads, and Sindiani 2020 used 60-70% loads with 8-rep sets. The error is about 1 rep, smallest in early reps and largest at light loads. In the deadlift, a 20% velocity loss is barely perceived (Shaw 2023).

On bias direction, the evidence leans toward PASS:
- the tie-break;
- the squat velocity change being underestimated (Sindiani);
- deadlift proximity to the threshold being underestimated (Shaw).

Halperin's underprediction of reps to failure measures a different construct. It would act only through early technique stops near failure, so it is a weak counterweight at 3-5 RIR. The size of the bias for this athlete is unknown.

At high RIR, PASS may simply be correct. HOLD ≤1 absorbs isolated noise, so clustered HOLDs, or their absence at H2 as loads rise, are where the signal lies. Free-weight squat velocity loss maps poorly to proximity (Jukic 2023), which also caps what any added slowdown log can show.

Remedy: logging only, judgment unchanged. Add one optional field on hard and focus sets: 'first rep that visibly slowed, or none'.

What I checked: the spec text, plus fresh reads of Shaw 2023, Jukic 2023 EJAP and Halperin 2022. I could not re-fetch Dello Iacono 2023 or Sindiani 2020: IJSPP returned a fetch error, ScienceDirect returned empty, and the web-search budget was used up. For those two I relied on the evidence pack's abstract-level verification.

Confirmed:
(1) The tie-break exists. Spec line 469: "감속인지 애매하면 감속으로 기록하지 않는다" (if it is unclear whether there was a slowdown, do not record one). Under §11.3, a NORMAL stop with 0 reps short is PASS, so borderline sets do resolve to PASS.
(2) Shaw 2023 abstract: n=17, deadlift at 60/80% 1RM, 10.49% probability vs 50% chance. Participants underestimated how close they were to 20% velocity loss (VL). That implies late stops, meaning missed slowdowns. This is lenient-direction evidence.
(3) Jukic 2023 EJAP: 46 trained lifters, free-weight back squat at 70/80/90%. The VL-to-%reps relationship had unacceptable between-session agreement, and mean absolute prediction error was above 10%. As cited.
(4) Halperin 2022: underprediction of 0.95 reps (CI 0.17-1.73), I² 97.9%. As cited.

Fidelity problems:
(a) Bullet 1 has no citation and only partly holds. Population rep tables (Nuzzo) put H3 at about 82% at roughly 5 reps in reserve (RIR), where a visible slowdown is unlikely. But the athlete's own anchor (92.5x3 at RPE 9-10) puts the current H2 of 90 kg at about 87% and roughly 2 RIR. Velocity loss there can be visible by rep 2. The claim should be split: plausible for H3 and V, not for H2 unless strength has risen since that anchor.
(b) Halperin is used out of scope as the counterweight for 'lens A'. It measures prediction of reps to failure, not perception of a velocity drop. In REF5 it could only act through the anticipatory 'stop before a rep that can't hold technique' rule, which matters near 0-1 RIR, not at 3-5 RIR. Halperin's own moderators also show better accuracy with 12 or fewer reps and with heavier loads, which is exactly REF5's context. The only signed evidence on the squat and deadlift (Sindiani: squat velocity change underestimated; Shaw: deadlift proximity to the VL threshold underestimated) points toward missed slowdowns. So the literature leans toward lens B (bias toward PASS). It is unconfirmed for this athlete, not evenly balanced.
(c) Dello Iacono needs two caveats. It is bench only. Error was largest at LIGHT loads (<50%, β 0.89), and heavier loads (β 0.63-0.84) were only worse than 50-60%. The authors also judge perceived VL usable at 50-90% when a 1-rep error is acceptable. Sindiani's error grew across reps from about 5.8 points at rep 2, so in 2-3-rep sets perception error is at its smallest. The stronger low-resolution argument is the floor effect: little true VL exists to detect in short sets, and the tie-break and 'obvious' threshold then default those sets to PASS. Perception error is the weaker part.
(d) Jukic cuts both ways. Even an accurately perceived slowdown maps poorly to proximity in the free-weight squat, which limits how much any slowdown tag can add.
(e) Minor: §22.4.8 concerns the lack of a max test, not information lost by the binary judgment. Only §22.4.7 fits.

Recommendation: it stays inside the fixed constraints. It is logging only, the judgment does not read it, and it uses no RIR, AMRAP or tests. Its expected value is modest. The only feedback-training evidence (Lazarus 2021) used light loads and a device, and Jukic limits how a logged slowdown can be interpreted. The 'first slowed rep' field and the 3-level tag overlap, so one is enough.

**반박 검토(applicability)**: holds-with-revision · 수정 확신 medium · 권고 유지 False

수정 주장: REF5's 'obvious slowdown' call (§11.1-11.3) has low resolution: it discards graded velocity information, as §22.4.7 already concedes.

- On REF5's own rules, the tie-break ('if ambiguous, record no slowdown') and the 'obvious' threshold fix the direction for borderline sets toward NORMAL, which becomes PASS when no reps are missing.
- The most applicable perception evidence points the same way. Velocity change is under-detected in the squat (Sindiani 2020, 60-70% loads) and in the deadlift (Shaw 2023, n=17).
- Underprediction of reps to failure (Halperin 2022) can only act through the stop-before-failure rule, which rarely fires at about 2-5 reps in reserve on 2-3-rep sets. It is a weak counterweight.
- What is unknown is how often sets fall in the borderline zone, and so the size of the net effect. The direction for borderline sets is not in doubt.
- Bench-only evidence that perception worsens above 60% 1RM (Dello Iacono 2023) does not transfer cleanly. The one squat-specific heavy-load study found 80% loads perceived accurately for large velocity losses (Dello Stritto 2025). So the gap is probably concentrated in subtle slowdowns.
- At H3 and the focus loads, PASS may simply be correct. H2 (3x2 at 90 kg, 2.5 kg under the athlete's 92.5x3 at RPE 9-10 anchor) may sit near 2 reps in reserve, where visible slowdowns are plausible.
- The errors are asymmetric. A missed slowdown yields PASS. A false-positive obvious slowdown mid-set ends the set, and deficits summed across sets can reach FAIL.
- HOLD ≤1 absorbs isolated noise, so clustered HOLDs are the signal.

Remedy within the fixed constraints: no new input field and no graded tag. Both are barred: §3.2 forbids velocity-grade input, and §26.2 treats any new user-entered item as a protocol change. If extra resolution is wanted, use one operational habit: free-text notes in the existing per-exercise memo (e.g. 'S2 rep 2 slightly slow'), or occasional plain video kept outside the app, with the judgment unchanged.

I checked the claim against docs/ref5-program-spec.md (§3.2, §11.1-11.3, §21.1, §22.4, §26.2, §27.4), the engine (packages/core/src/program-engine/ref5.ts:555-604) and the REF5 exercise card in the web UI (web/src/features/workout-log/ui/workout-exercise-card.tsx).

What holds:
- The weakness is real. The spec admits it in §22.4.7, which says the binary call discards velocity and day-to-day margin information.
- The tie-break is quoted correctly. §11.1 says "감속인지 애매하면 감속으로 기록하지 않는다" (if it is unclear whether a rep slowed, do not record a slowdown).
- In the engine, NORMAL with a deficit of 0 gives PASS, so a borderline set does end up as PASS.
- The idea that HOLD ≤1 absorbs noise matches §21.1.

What needs revision:

1. The bias direction is less open than the claim says. On REF5's actual rules, two things point toward PASS: the tie-break and the "obvious" threshold. They operate on any set where perception is in doubt. The most directly relevant perception evidence also points toward missed slowdowns: Sindiani 2020 for the squat and Shaw 2023 for the deadlift both show velocity change is under-detected. Lens A's counterweight, Halperin's underprediction of reps to failure, can only work through §11.1 rule 1 (stop before a rep you can't keep in form). That is a prediction of failure, and with about 2-5 reps in reserve on 2-3-rep sets it should rarely fire. Underpredicting by about 1 rep does not make a lifter skip a planned rep. So the direction for borderline sets is fixed by the rule. What is still unknown is how often sets land in that borderline zone, and so how big the net effect is on window outcomes.

2. Bullet 1 overgeneralizes. The athlete's own anchor is 92.5x3 at RPE 9-10. H2 is now 3x2 at 90 kg, only 2.5 kg lighter, so at that anchor H2 could sit at about 2 reps in reserve. The evidence pack itself puts the hard slots at roughly 2-5 RIR. At the low end of that range, a visible slowdown on rep 2 is plausible. "End before a visible slowdown would normally appear" fits H3 and the focus sets better than H2. The BP and PULL percentages also use control REFs, which §4 says are not 1RMs.

3. "Worse above 60% 1RM" comes from bench press only (Dello Iacono 2023). The one squat-specific heavy-load study in the pack, Dello Stritto 2025, found perception was most accurate at 80% 1RM with 40% velocity loss, and least reliable at 60% with 20% loss. That cuts against extending the bench result to REF5 squats. The resolution problem is probably concentrated in subtle slowdowns, not obvious ones.

4. The call is not purely PASS vs HOLD. The first obvious slowdown ends the set, and the engine sums deficits across all sets (≥2 = FAIL). A false-positive slowdown in the middle of a set can therefore produce a FAIL, which feeds the 2-consecutive-FAIL decrease and forced micro. A false negative only produces PASS. The costs of the two error types are asymmetric.

5. Citation slip: §22.4.8 is about having no max test, not about the binary judgment. Only §22.4.7 applies.

Why the recommendation fails:

(a) The 3-level slowdown tag (none / slight / obvious) is a velocity-grade input. Spec §3.2 explicitly lists "AMRAP, RPE, RIR, 속도 등급 입력" (velocity-grade input) as things that must not be added to REF5. It also brings back the graded subjective scale the athlete rejected as ambiguous (C10).

(b) §26.2 counts any new item the user enters as a protocol change ("사용자가 입력하거나 앱이 자동 계산하는 항목"), even if the judgment never reads it. So labelling the new per-exposure field "operational" is wrong.

(c) The recommendation bundles two new inputs plus a filming habit, which breaks the one-change-at-a-time rule.

(d) The field "first rep that visibly slowed" is either redundant with CLEAR_SLOWDOWN, for obvious slowdowns, or contradicts §11.1 rule 2 when logged alongside NORMAL. Paying closer attention to slowdowns could also shift the lifter's "obvious" threshold partway through a window, which hurts comparability.

(e) No source in the pack shows that plain video, without numeric feedback, calibrates velocity perception. Lazarus 2021 used augmented feedback.

A compliant option exists: the per-exercise memo already appears in REF5 sessions (the memo chip in workout-exercise-card.tsx is not gated by exercise.ref5). Free-text notes there, or occasional plain video kept outside the app, would add no input field and no grade scale.

## C10 · measurement · weakness · 확신 high · 변경 등급 doc-or-display

Control REF is a deterministic multiple of the working standard (+~3.15 per H3 increment), not an estimate of capacity. SQ, BP and PULL REFs (107.2 / 104.1 / 111.1) already exceed the last demonstrated maxima (~103 / ~100 / 110), even though working loads are still below the June 3x3 loads. With no max test, AMRAP, RIR or velocity device, nothing ties REF back to true 1RM, so REF cannot verify attainment of G1. Both lenses treat this as a knowingly accepted cost of C9/C10 that the spec itself acknowledges, not a hidden bug.

**근거**

- Internal record: current REFs vs reference maxima. Spec §6.3, §20 and §22.4.8 acknowledge that REF is not a 1RM and cannot prove 175 kg. §21.1 warns that relaxing the judgment would only inflate REF.
- Nuzzo et al. 2024, Sports Med 54(2):303-321 (952 tests, 7,289 people): the between-person SD of reps at a given %1RM is large, so population mappings cannot calibrate one individual's REF. https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40279-023-01937-7
- Grgic et al. 2020: trained 1RM tests are highly reliable (ICC 0.98, CV 3.3%). That is the information C9 gives up. https://link.springer.com/article/10.1186/s40798-020-00260-z
- Coaching contrast: the 5/3/1 7th-week protocol (AMRAP at 100% TM; under 3 reps lowers the TM) and Tactical Barbell's 6-12-week retests give a periodic reality check that REF5 lacks by user constraint. https://liftvault.com/resources/7th-week-protocol/ ; https://www.liftosaur.com/programs/tactical-barbell-operator

**추론**

These are record and spec facts, so confidence is high. It matters because the user's KPI is e1RM per kg of bodyweight, and showing REF as if it were a 1RM would mislead. The standard fixes (tests, AMRAP, RIR) are excluded by the user's constraints. The only internal calibration signal left is the first cluster of HOLD/FAIL on hard sets.

**반박 검토(evidence)**: holds-with-revision · 수정 확신 high · 권고 유지 True

수정 주장: Control REF is a fixed multiple of the direct working standard: SQ REF = 104 x H3 / 82.5, and likewise for the other lifts. A 2.5 kg H3 step adds about +3.15 to SQ REF, about +3.06 to BP REF and about +3.09 to PULL REF. The multipliers were anchored at plan start to the user's input 1RM estimates (104 / 101 / 108), so REF is an index of the working standard, not an independent capacity estimate.

Current REFs are 107.2 / 104.1 / 111.1. They sit above the old reference values, but only one of those values is a demonstrated maximum:
- SQ ~103 is an e1RM estimated from 92.5x3 at RPE 9-10.
- BP ~100-101 is an input estimate.
- PULL 110 total is the only real 1RM, and it is the last test, not necessarily the best (+40 kg was reached at a bodyweight of 65-75 kg).

PULL REF also inherits the bodyweight logged as a constant 75 kg. Meanwhile SQ working loads (H3 85, H2 90) are still below the earlier 92.5x3 load.

Without a max test, AMRAP, RIR or a velocity device, the program has only one-sided, coarse ties to capacity:
- HOLD/FAIL on hard sets flags 'too heavy'.
- Epley e1RM from non-failure logged sets on the stats page is a downward-biased lower bound.

Neither can confirm a 175 kg 1RM, so REF cannot verify G1. The spec acknowledges this in §6.3, §20, §22.4 #8 and §21.1. It is an accepted cost of C9/C10, not a hidden bug.

VERIFIED
- REF formula. packages/core/src/program-engine/ref5.ts deriveRef5ControlRefs (lines 338-342) computes sqKg = 104*H3/82.5, bpKg = 101*BP/82.5 and pullTotalKg = 108*PULLtotal/87.5. Spec §6.3 (docs/ref5-program-spec.md:219-233) gives the same formulas.
- Step size. 104*2.5/82.5 = 3.15 per H3 step.
- Current values. From the handoff/DB state (H3 85, BP 85, PULL total 90): 107.15 / 104.06 / 111.09, which matches the claim.
- Spec acknowledgements. All cited acknowledgements exist:
  - §4/§6.3: 'not 1RM/e1RM/TM; do not describe as %1RM'
  - §20 (line 797): cannot confirm SQ 175 from REF or REF/BW
  - §22.4 #8: no max test, so REF cannot prove the long-term goal
  - §21.1 (line 825): relaxing the judgment only inflates REF
- Nuzzo 2024. The Springer abstract confirms 952 tests and 7,289 people, failure-only tests, and mean and SD both falling as %1RM rises.
- Grgic 2020. Trained ICC 0.98, CV 3.3% (confirmed in the pack).
- LiftVault 7th-week protocol. Re-fetched and confirmed: 70/80/90% x5, then AMRAP at 100% TM. Fewer than 3 reps lowers the TM; more than 5 allows raising it. It is a TM check, not a 1RM test.
- Tactical Barbell 6-12-week retest. Confirmed via Liftosaur and LiftVault.

OVERSTATEMENTS AND REVISIONS
1. 'Last demonstrated maxima (~103/~100/110)' overstates the record.
   - SQ 103 is an e1RM from 92.5x3 at RPE 9-10 (constraints B1), not a demonstrated max.
   - No source for BP ~100 was found. The constraints sheet lists BP 101 as an input 1RM.
   - Only PULL 110 (75+35) was tested. Historical +40 kg at a bodyweight of 65-75 kg means the best total may be 105-115.
   - PULL REF depends on a bodyweight that is probably a default 75 kg, so the PULL comparison is itself unreliable.
   - 'Below the June 3x3 loads' could be checked only for SQ (92.5x3 in TB Operator per the handoff). BP and PULL June loads were not found in any accessible doc.
2. 'Nothing ties REF back to true 1RM' is too absolute, and the claim's own reasoning contradicts it.
   - HOLD/FAIL is a one-sided 'too heavy' check.
   - The app's stats page computes Epley e1RM (packages/core/src/stats/e1rm.ts) from logged sets. Because the sets stop before failure and Nuzzo's tables are failure-based, this is a downward-biased lower bound.
   - So the app shows two capacity proxies that bracket true capacity with opposite biases: REF about 107 and Epley about 93-96 from 85x3 / 90x2.
   - The correct statement is: nothing two-sided or precise ties REF to 1RM, and nothing can confirm 175.
3. 'Not an estimate of capacity' is normatively correct per the spec. But REF was anchored to the user's input 1RM estimates (constraints appendix: SQ 104, BP 101, PULL 108). It is therefore a 1RM estimate carried forward under a constant-relative-intensity assumption, which PASS outcomes cannot validate in either direction.
4. Nuzzo 'cannot calibrate' is too strong. Population tables give a wide-uncertainty estimate, not none. The pack reports between-person SD of about 2.5 reps at 80%. They are also failure-based, while REF5 sets are not, which makes them doubly indirect. The supporting point stands, at a lower strength than 'cannot'.

RECOMMENDATION CHECK
The recommendation stays inside the user's constraints: display only, no tests, AMRAP or RIR, and no protocol element changed. Caveats:
- The status panel already shows a disclaimer: 'REF5 runtime uses no 1RM/e1RM, TM...' (web/src/widgets/plans-manage-screen/ref5-status-panel.tsx:156). The REF chips under 'Control REFs' have no kg unit. The change is therefore a relabel or addition, not new disclosure.
- The ~103 reference must be labelled as a June e1RM estimate (92.5x3 at RPE 9-10), not as a 'demonstrated max'. Otherwise the display misleads in the other direction.
- 'First HOLD/FAIL cluster as calibration' is weak. The pack shows:
  - squat slowdown perception is coarse (Shaw 2023; Jukic 2023 EJAP: VL-%reps is unreliable between sessions in the free-weight squat)
  - recovery confounds such as sleep, deficit and stress shift outcomes
  - the RIR at the PASS/HOLD boundary is unknown (roughly 1-4)
  The cluster marks where the working standard meets the slowdown threshold under current recovery, not a 1RM value.

**반박 검토(applicability)**: holds-with-revision · 수정 확신 high · 권고 유지 True

수정 주장: Control REF is the working standard rescaled into start-calibration units (§5.3 inverse coefficients; §6.3 REF = 104·H3/82.5 etc., +~3.15 per H3 step). It equals the e1RM reference at plan start, then moves only with INCREASE/decrease decisions. Current REFs are SQ 107.2, BP 104.1 and PULL 111.1, above the reference values (~103 SQ e1RM from 92.5×3 @ RPE 9–10; ~100 BP conservative estimate; 110 PULL actual single). Passing the reference after two increments is arithmetic and says nothing either way about capacity. Current BP (85 3×3) and PULL (+15) loads roughly equal earlier working loads; only SQ is below the 92.5×3 top set. PULL REF also depends on a logged bodyweight that is probably a 75 kg default. With no max test, AMRAP, RIR or velocity device (C9/C10 and user principles), nothing ties REF to true 1RM. The app's Epley e1RM from non-failure sets gives only lower bounds (~93–99). A first HOLD/FAIL cluster shows how close a working load is to the slowdown point, not the 1RM, and is confounded by recovery. REF therefore cannot verify G1. The spec accepts this cost knowingly (§6.3, §20, §22.4.8), and §21.1 bars relaxing judgment, which would only inflate REF. Display fix within §26.1: keep REF labelled as a training-load index (the panel already disclaims 1RM), and show REF change since plan start and the acquisition rate (§18). Any historical 1RM estimate stays outside REF5 plan and runtime state, labelled as stale, and is never presented as a ratio to REF.

The core claim checks out against the spec, the engine and the record.

What checks out:
- REF formula. Spec §6.3 and engine ref5.ts:340-341 give SQ REF = 104·H3/82.5, BP REF = 101·BP/82.5 and PULL REF = 108·total/87.5.
- Current values. H3 85 gives 107.15, BP 85 gives 104.06 and PULL total 90 gives 111.09. One H3 step adds 104·2.5/82.5 = 3.15.
- Spec acknowledgements. §6.3 says REF is not a 1RM, e1RM or TM and must not be explained as %1RM. §20 says REF or REF/BW cannot confirm 175 kg. §22.4.8 lists this as a known weakness. §21.1 says relaxing the judgment only inflates REF.
- Citations. The Nuzzo 2024 and Grgic 2020 figures match the evidence pack.

Revisions needed:

(1) The "demonstrated maxima" are mostly estimates, and REF passing them is mostly arithmetic. Only PULL 110 was an actual single, used for the v1.4 calibration (docs/ref5-v1.4-oap-proposal.md:7,86). SQ ~103 is an e1RM from 92.5×3 @ RPE 9–10, range 102–105. BP ~100 was a deliberately conservative input: "85×3×3 무난 + TM drift correction" (web/docs/asymptote-hybrid-v0.5-proximity-patch.md:83-85). The §5.3 inverse coefficients set the starting REF at or just below that reference by construction: SQ floor2.5(103·82.5/104) = 80, giving REF 100.8. So any second INCREASE pushes REF above the reference automatically. REF exceeding the reference shows the judgment loop has moved it, not that it overstates capacity.

(2) "Working loads still below June 3x3 loads" holds only for SQ, and even there the comparison is with a single 92.5×3 top set; a June squat 3×3 load was not found. BP focus 85 equals June's 85×3×3. PULL +15 is about equal to the "+15~16×3" in the same doc.

(3) The claim misses a PULL-specific problem. PULL REF uses the target total, which includes the logged bodyweight of 75, probably a default. The actual load lifted and REF/BW are both uncertain.

(4) "The only internal calibration signal is the first HOLD/FAIL cluster" overstates it.
- The stats page already shows Epley e1RM from logged sets (packages/core/src/stats/e1rm.ts). On REF5's non-failure sets these are lower bounds: H2 90×2 gives 96, H3 85×3 gives 93.5, PULL 90×3 gives 99. So the app shows two diverging strength numbers, one below the reference and one above it, and neither is a 1RM.
- A HOLD/FAIL cluster tells you how close a working load is to the slowdown point. Turning that into a 1RM needs the same population reps-%1RM mapping the claim rejects (Nuzzo).
- It is also confounded by recovery (C2), training gaps and the knee. §21.1 pre-registers that low success is read first as a stimulus or recovery deficit.

Recommendation: within bounds, with caveats.
- Changing the wording is a §26.1 protocol-preserving change. The web panel already labels these values "Control REFs / auxiliary caps", shows REF chips without kg, and carries the disclaimer "REF5 runtime uses no 1RM/e1RM, TM" (ref5-status-panel.tsx:156,239-243). So the "shown as if 1RM" risk is already partly handled.
- Showing ~103 beside REF needs care.
  - The value must not be stored in plan params or runtime state (§5.1, §5.3, §26.3). A new user-entered item would trigger §26.2 review.
  - A frozen four-month-old estimate next to a rising REF invites the %1RM reading that §6.3 forbids, and C9 means it can never be refreshed.
  - A safer display is REF change since plan start plus the §18 acquisition rate. Any historical reference belongs in the personal-profile or stats context, labelled as a stale estimate.
- The recommendation changes no protocol element and violates no fixed constraint.

## C11 · measurement · weakness · 확신 high · 변경 등급 operational

Bodyweight is effectively unmeasured: 75.00 kg is logged in 28 of 29 sessions (76 on 10/14), and the body_measurement table has 0 rows. As a result, none of these can be computed:
- the G1 KPI (e1RM ÷ bodyweight);
- the §21 REF ÷ 7-day-bodyweight trend;
